### Page 2

世界経済：AI・防衛関連需要を受け景況感は好調。一方でインフレ圧力の広がりも確認
 9月のグローバル製造業PMIは53.0（8月52.3）と堅調。非製造業は54.5（8月53.6）と40か月ぶりの高水準
― 製造業はAI・防衛関連投資需要を受け生産、新規受注ともに上昇。非製造業も幅広い業種で業況が改善
― 一方、仕入・販売価格指数はともに上昇。エネルギーや輸送価格上昇によるインフレ圧力の広がりを示唆
 国・地域別では米国・ユーロ圏が製造業・非製造業ともに改善。とりわけ米国の好調が目立つ
― 米国ではAI関連支出の拡大に伴い機械・設備投資および生産が増加。非製造業においても情報通信業が突出して好調
― ユーロ圏でも、AI投資に支えられたIT関連サービスが特に力強く拡大。専門・商業サービスも改善
1
製造業の景況感（購買担当者景気指数） 非製造業の景況感（購買担当者景気指数）
（注） 日米欧：S&P Global、中：国家統計局。購買担当者景気指数：企業に対するアン
ケート調査結果から作成した指数。新規受注や生産高、雇用、出荷遅延などの指数

に一定のウエイトを掛けて算出。50が景気拡大・縮小の分岐点
（出所）S&P Global、中国国家統計局より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
（注） 日米欧：S&P Global、中：国家統計局。購買担当者景気指数（非製造業）：企業に対
するアンケート調査結果から作成した指数。50が景気拡大・縮小の分岐点

### Page 3

金融市場概況：投資家心理が悪化し、日米とも株安・金利低下
 AIインフラ投資への信用不安の高まりから、日米株価はハイテク株を中心に下落
― 米新興クラウドサービスの社債価格急落が株安の契機に。原油高に伴うインフレ懸念も下押し圧力に
― 投資家心理が悪化するなかで、安全資産とされる日米国債に資金が流入、前月までの金利上昇基調は一巡
 ドル円は、1ドル＝150円台後半で方向感の乏しい展開
― 日米金利とも低下し、日米金利差は概ね横ばい圏で推移
― 原油高が輸入増を通じて円安要因となる一方、日米株安に伴うリスクオフや仏情勢悪化に伴うユーロ安は円高要因に
2

米国：インフレ率は高止まり、油価再加速で当面は物価高が続く懸念
 原油高や関税の影響から物価は加速
―9月のコアCPIは前月比＋0.3％（年率＋4.1％）と8月（前月比＋0.3％、年率＋3.5％）から伸びが拡大
― コアサービスでは住居費や輸送サービスなどが押し上げに寄与。コア財については、衣料品や玩具などが上昇。関税引
き上げに伴う価格転嫁が続いている模様
 足元で原油価格が再加速していることを踏まえれば、当面は物価の上振れリスクが残存する懸念
― シカゴ連銀が公表する週次の小売物価指標は9月後半から10月初めにかけて上昇しており、物価の高止まりを示唆
3
（出所） 米労働省より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
項目別コアCPI 週次小売物価（シカゴ連銀）

（出所） シカゴ連銀より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

米国：足元の消費はガソリン高や関税に伴う物価上昇で足踏みも、年末商戦は堅調な見通し
 消費は足踏み。ガソリン価格高騰や関税に伴う物価高が家計の購買力を下押し
―9月の実質小売売上高は前月比▲0.5％と減少。ガソリン小売が減少したほか、自動車や衣料品も低調に推移
 物価高は逆風も、2026年の年末商戦は堅調予想
― 米マスターカードは、実質小売売上高（除くガソリン・自動車）は前年比＋2.7％と、前年（同＋2.1％）から伸びが拡大する
と予想。雇用環境の改善などを背景に幅広い所得層で安定した消費がみられると指摘
4
（注） ガソリンはCPIを用いて実質化。その他は、8、9月のみ各品目に対応するCPIを用

米国：雇用は減速も、AI関連需要が下支え
 9月の非農業部門雇用者数は前月差＋2.9万人と市場予想（同＋9.0万人）を下回る結果。7月・8月も下方修正
― 民間部門が前月差＋4.6万人（8月同＋8.9万人）と増勢が鈍化したほか、政府部門が同▲1.7万人と再びマイナス転化
― 民間部門では、旺盛なAI投資需要などを背景に、製造や建設、建築・エンジニアリングなどが増加基調を維持したもの
の、情報、金融、専門サービスなどの高賃金セクターが減少
― 時間当たり賃金の伸びは前月比＋0.1％（8月同＋0.3％）と鈍化。こちらも市場予想（同＋0.3％）を下回る結果
― 総じて冴えない結果ながら、3カ月平均では＋5万人程度を維持しており、雇用は減速しつつも失速には至らず

5
（出所） 米労働省より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

### Page 7

米国：対人型業種の雇用に底堅さ。労働参加率上昇で失業率は小幅上昇
 対人接触度の高い業種では雇用に底堅さ、AIによる代替が進みにくい可能性
― ヘルスケアや建設、飲食サービスなど、対人接触機会の多い業種では雇用が増加する一方、経理・会計サービス、ITシ
ステム開発などでは低調
 失業率は4.2％と小幅悪化も引き続き低水準を維持
― 主に16-24歳の若年層およびプライムエイジ層の労働参加率の上昇が押し上げに寄与した模様
6
（注）1.バブルの大きさは各産業の雇用者数
2. 対人接触度は、O*NETの職業別のPhysical Proximity（業務上、他者と物理的に近
接する度合い）を、各産業の職業別雇用者構成で加重平均した指数
3.コロナ禍からの雇用回復の影響を除くため、雇用者数の変化率は2023年末を起点
に算出
（出所） 米労働省、セントルイス連銀より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

米国：生産は底堅く推移。AI・防衛が投資財生産をけん引、半導体増産も寄与
 2026年8月の鉱工業生産は前年比＋1.4％と底堅い結果
― 半導体関連の生産が引き続きけん引。生産能力が2022年対比1.7倍に拡大するなど生産体制の増強が背景。事実、主
要半導体メーカーによる工場新設・増強を受け、MOSロジック（GPU、CPUなど演算用）の量産が本格化
 AI・防衛分野を中心に投資財生産も堅調さを維持
― 投資関連財はAI・防衛が押し上げる形で同＋4.7％と増勢継続。国防・宇宙関連設備は同＋6.1％ と全体を上回る伸び。
地政学リスクの高まりを背景に軍用機やミサイル・防空システムなどの生産体制強化が図られている模様
7

生活費
期待より悪い 期待通り 期待より良い
（シェア）
米国：中間選挙を目前に住宅市場は軟調、アフォーダビリティへの不満が共和党への逆風に
 住宅ローン金利は足元で再び上昇
― 借入コストの負担増により、足元の住宅ローン申請件数は減少
 物価上昇が生活コストを押し上げるなか、金利上昇による住宅取得環境の悪化も家計の不満を助長する要因に
― トランプ大統領の生活費への政策対応は約7割が「期待より悪い」と評価しており、支持率は低下基調
―11月に控える中間選挙では、アフォーダビリティへの対応が引き続き主要な論点

8

欧州：G7は原油・ディーゼルの協調放出で合意。米国の輸出制限リスクは後退
9
 G7はIEAを通じ4カ月で1億バレルの石油備蓄放出に合意。初期20日間はディーゼルを前倒し放出
― 背景にディーゼル価格高騰。中間選挙を控えたトランプ大統領は、禁輸も辞さない姿勢を強調（米国は世界需要の4.9％
を供給）。米国からの輸出が欧州の需要を支える一方、米国よりも潤沢な在庫を保有する欧州に圧力をかけた格好
― 欧州は石油製品消費に占めるディーゼル比率が高く、価格高騰を回避する思惑も協調放出の合意を後押し
― 合意ではG7域内でのエネルギー輸出制限回避も確認され、米国によるEU向け禁輸リスクはひとまず後退
石油製品の製品別消費割合の国際比較
（出所）Energy Institute より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
EUの石油製品消費はディーゼル・軽油が中心

### Page 11

欧州：ディーゼル需給逼迫は残存。欧州のインフレ上振れ要因に
10
 ロシアのディーゼル輸出は足元で急減。中東からの輸出減、中国の輸出停止も重なり、世界需要の約6％が失われた格好
―G7の1億バレル放出で当面の供給不安は緩和するものの、構造的な供給不足の解消には至らず
―IEAも、中東からの原油輸出は回復する一方、石油製品の供給制約はなお深刻と指摘。ディーゼル需給の逼迫が続け
ば、輸送・物流コストを通じたインフレ圧力が残存
 欧州では9月HICPが再加速。ディーゼル高が輸送・物流コストを通じて追加的なインフレ上振れ要因となるリスク
―9月消費者物価（HICP）は前年比＋3.8％（前月：同＋3.2％）と伸び加速。エネルギー価格の上昇が主因
今般の備蓄放出量と停止中のディーゼル輸出
（注） 世界需要およびロシア・中国の輸出量は2025年実績。中東は2026年8月実績を参
考に、輸出量を前年の4分の1としてシェアを概算
（出所）EIA、IEA、OPEC、各種報道より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

（出所）Eurostat より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

欧州：EU・中国はハイブリッド車の輸入制限で暫定合意。同様の措置は他の品目に広がる可能性
11
 EUと中国は、貿易不均衡の是正措置について暫定合意。中国からのハイブリッド車輸入は従来見通しの半分以下に
―EUが中国産EVに追加関税を賦課した2024年以降、追加関税の対象とならないハイブリッド車輸入が急増
― 欧州委員会の試算では、今回の措置によって2030年までの4年間で数百万台の輸入を削減見込み。直近では、EUが輸
入するハイブリッド車の約半分を中国車が占めており、域内自動車市場に一定のインパクト
 欧州委員会は、幅広い産業で貿易不均衡を是正する必要性を示唆。同様の措置が他の品目にも広がる可能性
― 欧州委員会のモニタリング対象（輸入増加と価格下落が併存する品目）は、中国からの輸入品が最多

欧州：2027年度予算審議が本格化。生活苦によるデモが拡大する中、財政赤字は高水準
12
 2027年度予算審議が本格化。財政赤字は総じて高止まり
― 一定の防衛費・エネルギー関連支出を免除する特例措置の適用後も、複数の国でEU財政基準で定める赤字幅を超過。

― フランスの学生によるデモを発端に、生活費の高騰や公共サービスの劣化に対する抗議活動は欧州各国に拡大。一部

欧州：長期金利上昇を受け、一部ECB高官は追加利上げの必要性低下を指摘
13
 フランスでは国内政治・財政面の不安から長期金利が上昇。イタリア・ギリシャなど周辺の高債務国にも波及
― フランス国債の対独利回り差は急拡大し、一時欧州債務危機（2012年）以来の水準に。その後、大統領選の有力候補と
目されるルペン氏（国民連合）が財政赤字を縮小する意欲を示し、拡大はひとまず一服
―ECBによる介入（国債買い入れ等）の可能性については、複数の高官が検討段階にないとの見方を示す
 長期金利の上昇は需要を抑制。ECB高官からは追加利上げの必要性が低下するとの見方も
― エネルギー高によるインフレ圧力と金利上昇による景気下押し圧力が併存。ECBの政策判断は一段と難しくなる可能性

中国：7～9月期の実質GDPは＋4.5％。インフラ投資の加速で、通年の成長率目標を確保へ
 7～9月期の実質GDP成長率は前年比＋4.5％。1～9月の累計で同＋4.6％と、通年目標「＋4.5～5.0％」の範囲内
―7～9月期は、純輸出が＋1.8％Ptと成長をけん引する一方、総資本形成は＋0.6％Ptまで縮小。内需の弱さ鮮明
― 中国政府は地方政府専項債の発行枠を追加（2,500億元）。インフラ投資を加速させ、成長率目標の達成を確保へ
 9月の小売は同＋1.1％と、前月（同＋0.4％）から加速。メモリー高騰が通信機器の売上額（同＋31.1％）を押し上げ
― 投資は同▲7.2％（前月：同▲10.6％） 。前年の反動増は限定的で、インフラ投資は同▲10.5％と2ケタ減が続く

― 生産は同＋5.5％（前月：同＋5.2％）。生産量では、産業用ロボット（同＋18.9％）や集積回路（同＋18.4％）が堅調

中国：政府は住宅ローンの利子補給で市場テコ入れ。人民元相場は緩やかな元高が継続
 中国政府が9/28、新規住宅ローン金利の年率1％分を補助する政策を発表。低迷が続く不動産市場をテコ入れ
― 対象物件の購入金額を150万元（3,500万円）以下に限定し、低中所得者層の住宅ローン負担を軽減する
― これにより、余剰在庫に苦しむ地方都市での実需を掘り起こし、市況のさらなる悪化に歯止めをかけたい考え
 人民元相場は、中国の貿易黒字が過去最高水準を記録する中、緩やかに元高が進行し1ドル＝6.6元台に突入
― 高水準の貿易黒字を背景とした人民元買いの実需が強いことから、当面は緩やかな元高が続く見込み
― ただ、米FRBの利上げで米中金利差が拡大しており、さらなる拡大が意識されれば元安に転じる可能性も

日本：旺盛なAI関連需要が製造業の景況感を押し上げ。利益計画は大幅上方修正
16
 大企業の業況判断は製造業が6四半期連続の改善。非製造業は悪化も一時的な悪天候が主因で変調の懸念は低い
― 業種別では、化学、石油・石炭製品、生産用機械等が改善。旺盛なAI関連需要や原油の供給不足懸念の後退等が背景

― とりわけ大企業・製造業では、輸出向け売上高の上方修正が顕著。上期の実績が大幅に上振れたことを反映したもの
― 世界的なAI関連需要の好調持続が見込まれるもと、下期も堅調な輸出が続けば、利益計画は一段の上方修正余地

― 積み上がった受注残を段階的に消化することで、設備投資が持続的に下支えされる公算が大きい

日本：コメ価格下落で企業物価は鈍化。もっとも、先行きは原油高を背景に再加速へ
18
 9月の国内企業物価はコメ価格の下落を主因に、前年比＋6.8％（前月：同＋7.6％）と鈍化
― 新米価格が昨年を下回ったこと等を背景に、コメ価格が大幅に下落。国内企業物価を▲0.75%Pt押し下げ
― 一方、3月来の原油高を背景に化学・プラスチック製品は高止まり。また、AI需要等を背景に非鉄金属も高い伸びを維持
 7月以降の原油高再燃を背景に、先行きの国内企業物価は再加速する見込み
― 原油価格高止まりにタンカー運賃の急騰が重なり、先行きの輸入物価は原油関連を中心に加速へ
― 国内企業物価は石油・石炭製品やラグを伴いつつ化学・プラスチック製品などを中心に再加速する見込み
（注） コメは玄米及び精米。食品関連は、飲食料品及び農林水産物（除くコメ）

米国債券市場：インフレ加速懸念で上昇後、AI関連株の下落を受け金利は低下
 10月の米10年国債利回りは、一時5.3％台まで上昇したものの、足元では5.0％前後まで低下
― 月初は、原油高等によるインフレ加速懸念を受け、長期金利は約24年ぶりの高水準となる5.3％台まで上昇
― 中旬以降は、AI関連株の先行きに対する慎重な見方から、安全資産である米国債への需要が拡大し、長期金利は低下
 今後も、中東情勢に伴う原油価格の変動やAI関連株の動向など、金利を左右する不確実性が多く、振れの大きい展開が続
く可能性

国内債券・為替：株安を背景に長期金利は低下。ドル円は150円台後半で横ばい圏
 長期金利は、株安を背景に低下。10年金利・30年金利とも、10月上旬から30bps以上の大幅低下
― 米新興クラウドサービスの社債価格急落が株安の契機となり、安全資産とされる日本国債に資金が流入
― 先行き、株式市場の混乱が続けば、短期的には一段の金利低下余地も。もっとも、日銀は四半期に一度のペースで利上
げを続けるとみられ、混乱一巡後の円金利は、再び緩やかな上昇基調に転じる見通し
 ドル円は150円台後半で方向感に欠ける展開。先行きも、150円台を中心とした円安水準が続く見込み
― 原油高は円安要因の一方、株安に伴うリスクオフや仏情勢悪化を受けたユーロ安は円高要因。目先は両者が綱引きへ

内外株式：AIインフラ投資への信用不安の高まりから、AI・半導体関連株を中心に下落

― 企業業績への期待から、10月初の日米株は上昇。S&P500は一時最高値を更新。日経平均も一時7万円台を回復
― その後は、米新興クラウドサービスの社債価格の急落を受け、AI・半導体関連株を中心に下落。原油価格の上昇に伴

うインフレ懸念も下押し圧力に。もっとも、S&P500はヘルスケア関連株等への資金シフトが進み、底堅く推移
 AIインフラ投資に対する信用不安は残存。原油価格の上昇も重石となり、日米株は軟調な推移が続くと予想
― 金利が高水準で推移する中、AIインフラの過剰投資懸念が強まり株価を下押しするリスクには留意が必要

## Document 085: 2603長榮 統一

Source PDF: all report pdf/2603長榮 統一.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-18

### Page 1

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  1

2026/10/12 投資速報

營收走勢圖

研究員/王建智 MICHAELWANG82121188@UNI-
PSG.COM

研究部對於長榮看法轉趨正向，係因：(1) 3Q26 受惠貨櫃航運
旺季及運價回升，平均運價漲幅明顯高於燃油成本增幅， 帶動獲
利顯著成長。(2) 4Q26 雖進入傳統淡季，惟北美農曆春節前備
貨 、歐洲貨量成長及亞洲區域航線旺季提供支撐，預期獲利雖低
於 3Q26，但仍優於 2Q26。(3) 2027 年雖面臨新船交付增加的供
給壓力， 但全球碼頭擴建速度落後船隊成長，加上塞港及紅海繞
航持續消耗有效運力，有助減緩新增供給對運價的衝擊， 北美及
歐洲航線長約運價亦預期高於 2026 年，支撐全年獲利。預估
2026/2027 年稅後 EPS 分別為45.21 元（+42.7%YoY）／27.89 元
（-38.3%YoY）。歷史本淨比區間為 0.4～1.8 倍，以 2027 年底
預估每股淨值 298.36 元為評價基礎，給予0.95 倍本淨比， 目標
價 283 元，將投資評等調升至買進。

重點摘要： 
1. 3Q26 運價漲幅高於燃油成本增幅，帶動獲利顯著成長 
長榮 3Q26 營收 1,467.76 億元，+39.6%QoQ/+51.4%YoY，主要受
惠第三季貨櫃航運傳統旺季， 歐美航線進入零售商備貨高峰， 帶
動貨運需求回升，加上紅海繞航、 全球港口壅塞持續消耗有效運
力，以及航商透過艙位控管調節供給，推升主要航線運價。7、8
月平均運費分別為 1,539 及1,577 美元／TEU， 參考9 月SCFI 月
均 3,657 點，+8.0%MoM， 推估9 月平均運費約 1,703 美元／TEU，
並假設 9 月貨量與 8 月持平，則 3Q26 平均運費約 1,606 美元／
TEU，+44.7%QoQ，貨量276 萬 TEU，-1.8%QoQ，營收成長主要由
運價上漲貢獻。

成本方面 ， 預估每噸耗油成本由2Q26 的 645 美元升至 714 美元 ，
+10.6%QoQ，假設季度耗油量持平，燃油費用增加約22 億元，惟
運價漲幅明顯高於燃油成本增幅 ， 營收增加帶來的獲利貢獻可望
大幅超過成本上升影響。預估 3Q26 稅後淨利 479.69 億元，
+199.2%QoQ/+120.6%YoY，稅後EPS 22.16 元。

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

圖一、各月平均運費、每噸耗油成本、貨量

資料來源：長榮，統一投顧整理 
2. 4Q26 進入傳統淡季，惟區域航線需求支撐營運 
第四季為貨櫃航運傳統淡季， 整體需求不如第三季旺季強勁， 惟
根據訂艙及裝載情況 ， 至11 月前貨量仍具支撐 。 北美航線方面 ，
美國主要港口進口貨櫃量維持高檔，零售庫存銷售比低於 1.3，
顯示零售商庫存水位相對偏低，仍有持續補貨需求，預期 11 月
貨量隨淡季趨緩 ，12 月則有農曆春節前備貨需求支撐 。 歐洲航線
方面 ，2026 年貨量成長優於預期 ， 雖運價自7 月後逐步回落 ， 但
紅海地緣政治風險尚未解除， 繞航仍持續消耗有效運力。 亞洲區
域航線第四季進入傳統旺季 ， 受惠中國加一帶動東南亞及南亞原
料、半成品運輸需求成長。此外，全球塞港造成約300 萬至 400
萬 TEU 有效運力損失，占全球總運力約 11%至 12%，搭配航商艙
位控管，有助支撐淡季運價。

預估 4Q26 營收 1,174.21 億元，-20.0%QoQ/+37.0%YoY，稅後淨
利 255.68 億元，-46.7%QoQ/+200.2%YoY，稅後 EPS 11.81 元。

3. 2027 年新船交付壓力增加，港口壅塞限制有效運力供給 
2027~2029 年為全球貨櫃船新船交付高峰，目前全球貨櫃船總運
力約3,400 萬TEU，至2030 年待交付新船運力超過1,400 萬TEU，
主流研調機構(Clarksons、Drewry)預估 2027 年名目運力供給增
速為 7.7~7.9%，供給成長壓力仍是 2027 年主要風險。惟名目運
力增加不代表有效運力等幅成長 ， 過去十年全球碼頭處理能力年
均成長約 2.7%，低於船隊運力年均成長約 5.4%，港口基礎設施
擴張速度落後船隊成長，塞港問題短期難以改善。紅海及蘇伊士
運河恢復通行亦為重要變數， 歐洲航線若恢復通行，單程約節省

### Page 3

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  3

7 天 、 往返約14 天 ， 但船舶集中抵港可能加劇碼頭壅塞 ， 抵銷部
分航程縮短釋出的運力。長榮截至 2030 年尚有 72 艘新船待交
付 ， 合計運力約89 萬 TEU， 並持續投資重要樞紐碼頭 ， 提升靠泊
效率及船隊調度彈性。

4. 2027 年歐美長約運價預期高於 2026 年，支撐全年獲利 
2027 年北美及歐洲航線長約運價預期均高於 2026 年，主要受惠
三項因素 。 首先 ，2026 年第三季貨櫃運價大幅上漲 ， 目前現貨運
價明顯高於前一年度長約議價時的水準 ， 為新年度合約價格調升
奠定基礎 。 其次 ， 全球港口處理能力擴張速度落後船隊運力成長 ，
加上紅海繞航及主要港口壅塞持續消耗有效運力 ， 航商供給調節
能力提升，有利維持長約議價能力。最後，歐美航線貨運需求仍
具支撐 ， 北美零售商庫存水位偏低 ， 歐洲航線貨量成長優於預期 ，
提供長約價格上調條件。歐洲航線新約自 2027 年初開始適用，
北美航線則自 5 月起生效 ， 預期新年度長約運價調升將逐步反映
於營收，並有助減緩現貨運價波動對獲利的影響，支撐 2027 年
營運表現。

5. 預估 2026/2027 年稅後 EPS 45.21/27.89 元，塞港及長約運
價調升支撐獲利高於歷史平均水準 
預估 2026/2027 年營收分別為 4,558.69／4,178.70 億元，分別
+20.3%YoY/-8.3%YoY，稅後淨利分別為 978.74／603.81 億元，
分別+42.7%YoY/-38.3%YoY，稅後 EPS 分別為 45.21 元／27.89
元。2026 年受惠第三季運價大幅上漲 ， 帶動全年獲利回升 。2027
年雖面臨新船交付增加及運價自高檔回落的壓力 ， 惟全球碼頭處
理能力成長落後船隊運力，加上塞港及紅海繞航限制有效供給，
歐美航線長約價格亦預期高於 2026 年 ， 有助減緩獲利下滑幅度 。

### Page 3

TSMC (2330.TW)
15 October 2026 Citi Research
3
Rather than now slowing down, it’s actually getting stronger
TSMC reported an upbeat 3Q26 result with robust 4Q26 outlook. It guided for
4Q26 revenue of US$52.3–54.3 billion, representing about 14% QoQ growth and
58% year-over-year growth at the midpoint (based on FX at NT$31.8 per USD).
4Q26 GM/OPM is expected at 65–67% and 56.5–58.5%. TSMC reiterated its 2026
capex to be US$60–64 billion and said investment from 2026 to 2028 will be

significantly higher than in the previous three years, reflecting strong multi-year
customer demand, including agentic AI. It is also worth noting that TSMC plans to
begin using the High-NA EUV technology around 2030 and develop a 12-inch
mask capability by 2031, when it expects the tools to offer sufficient maturity and
cost effectiveness. Management said this timing does not undermine its
confidence in technology leadership.
Management described AI-related demand as exceptionally strong, supported by

accelerators, networking chips, and rising demand for CPUs associated with
agentic AI. TSMC’s previously guided AI GPU/accelerator CAGR to be mid-to-high
50%, but now sees more upside ahead. Given rising concern on AI expanding
peaking out, the management said it is clear that its capacity planning strategy has
considered customer forecasts, data-center construction, power availability, and
other supply-chain constraints. Management still judges demand to be above

available supply and is working to close the gap, while emphasizing disciplined
planning to limit the risk of overbuilding.
Figure 1. TSMC – 3Q26 results comparison
© 2026 Citigroup Inc. No redistribution without Citigroup’s written permission.
Source: Citi Research, Bloomberg
Capacity expansion plan and allocation
During the earnings call, TSMC also mentioned that despite the tight capacity
supply, customers cannot generally sublet or auction wafer capacity, while there is

limited case that collaboration is through mask-license agreements. For the
advanced packaging, TSMC has announced its Global Foundries and VIS’
agreement on interposer as part of its backend supply strategy, intended to help
ensure customers have enough capacity to bring products to market; It also
reiterated that TSMC’s CoWoS remains as the leading customer’s choice and is
already in volume production at 5x reticle size, while it is developing 9x and larger

solutions and evaluating CoPoS.

### Page 4

TSMC (2330.TW)
15 October 2026 Citi Research
4
Reiterate Buy, best position in AI arena
Our earnings projection is largely unchanged for 2027 /2028 earnings. While its
capex expansion, overseas operation and N2 will likely be a margin dilution factor
initially, due to its cross-node leverage, high UTR and yield rate, we believe its GM
downside is manageable. Overall, we see strong advanced node demand into next
year and the long-term order visibility will be supported for higher capex cycle.  We

maintain our TP to NT$4,000, based on unchanged 25x of 2027 EPS estimate;
reiterate Buy.
Key TSMC earnings call recap
n4Q26 guidance
–Sales: US$52.3-54.3bn (+14% QoQ at the midpoint)
–GPM: 65-67% (assuming USDTWD=31.8)
–OPM: 56.5-58.5%
n2026 full-year & long-term guidance
–Full year sales: raise 2026 full-year USD-based sales guidance from more
than 40% growth to +44% growth
–Full year capex: retain its 2026 capex guidance at US$60-64bn (70-80%
advanced process, 10% specialty, 10-20% for advanced packaging, testing

and masks)
–GM outlook
•4Q GM to be impacted by N2 and overseas fab ramps, but partially
supported by productivity improvement, UTR enhancement and
optimizing capacity allocation
•GM dilution from overseas fab would be c2ppts impact for the full-year
2026; with more new capacity expansion in the US, the margin dilution
impact would widen to 3-4ppts in the next few years.
–Long-term outlook
•Retains 5-year AI sales CAGR (2024-2029) remains at high-50% or above

nCapacity planning
–TSMC currently has 20 fabs in total. It plans to add 13 fabs in Taiwan and add
5-6 overseas fabs.
–N2 capacity will double in 2027; it has also started preparing for A14 capacity
with volume production starting from 2028.
–US Arizona fab 2 construction is complete and begin volume production in
3Q27, ahead of schedule.
–JP fab 2 would produce N3 with mass production from 2028

Catalyst Watch on TSMC (2330.TW)
Direction: Upside
Duration: Within 90 Days (expires 06 Jan 2027)
Date Added: 07 Oct 2026
Catalyst: Thematic driven
TSMC will host 3Q26 earnings call on Oct 15. We expect TSMC to beat its recently raised guidance once again. Upbeat
guidance, robust demand for advanced nodes into next year, and a higher capex cycle should provide further upside
potential for the share price in the coming months.

### Page 9

TSMC (2330.TW)
15 October 2026 Citi Research
9
Bull/Bear: TSMC (2330.TW)

### Page 10

TSMC (2330.TW)
15 October 2026 Citi Research
10
TSMC
Company description
TSMC is the founder and leader of the dedicated IC foundry segment. The
company has built its reputation by offering advanced and "more-than-
Moore" wafer production processes and industry-leading manufacturing
efficiency. From its inception in 1987, TSMC has consistently offered the
foundry segment's leading technologies and TSMC-compatible design
services.
Investment strategy

We rate TSMC shares as Buy. We expect TSMC to deliver sales growth of
>40% in recent years thanks to strong growth from HPC/AI as well as stable
demand growth from smartphone, Io T, and automotive on tech migration and
a shortened replacment cycle. CPU outsourcing to TSMC offers further
growth potential. A rising cash dividend should also support the share price.
While TSMC has been increasing its capex meaningfully since 2009, its cost
structure remaisn competitive through continuous product mix

improvements, efficiency enhancements, and cost reductions.
Valuation
Our target price for TSMC shares of NT$4,000 is based on 25x our 2027E
EPS, higher than its three-year average forward PER, versus global peers’
average of 16-25x. We believe our target PER is justified by TSMC's leading
position in advanced process node and robust AI demand growth outlook.
Given its continuing leadership in the semiconductor foundry industry, we
believe TSMC’s stable order visibility and earnings outlook make it less

vulnerable to a potential global economic downturn than some of its peers.
Our target price equates to 2026E/27E P/E of ~ 36x/25x and 2026E/27E P/B of
~13x/10x.
Risks
Key downside risks that could impede the shares from achieving our target
price include: 1) weakness in the global semiconductor market; 2) larger-
than-expected margin contractions due to depreciation cost increases and
strong NTD; 3) competitors entering the foundry business; 4) longer-than-

expected digestion of inventory in the supply chain; and 5) a slowdown in
demand either due to a global economic downturn or a global trade
disruption triggered by tariffs.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

Implications — We believe the margin implications of a rising semi sales mix are well
understood by the market and leave our investment thesis intact. Our constructive
view is based on steady growth in existing businesses, supplemented by medium- to
long-term opportunities in humanoid robots, drones, and LEO satellites. We also

view Sinbon as a relatively defensive stock amid AI-related market volatility, given
its limited direct AI exposure and diversified business portfolio. The shares could
benefit from investor rotation toward companies with resilient underlying
fundamentals, while emerging applications provide additional support for the
longer-term growth outlook.

Sinbon
Valuation
Our TP for Sinbon is NT$370. We value Sinbon on PE to capture the company’s earnings growth prospects. We set our target
PE at 23x, which we set at a 20% premium to the historical average of 19x since 2018. We believe 23x is justified in view of
improving fundamentals with strong momentum of industrials segment, driving a clear margin expansion and solid earnings
growth in 2026E. Applying this to our 2H26-1H27E EPS forecast, we arrive at our target price of NT$370.

Risks
Key downside risks that could cause Sinbon's share to deviate from our target price include 1) slower-than-expected growth in
non-IT/non-consumer applications; 2) Intensified competition; 3) Raw material price hike and labor cost increases; 4) FX
volatility; 5) Greater-than-expected impact from the geopolitical conflict.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

buy recommendation and a Catalyst Watch/STV Downside call corresponds to a sell recommendation. Any stock not assigned
to a Catalyst Watch Upside, Catalyst Watch Downside, STV Upside, or STV Downside call is considered Catalyst Watch/STV

### Page 1

Powertech Technology

Up-cycle level of earnings to continue; 
reiterate Buy

Reiterate Rating: BUY | PO: 500.00 TWD | Price: 254.50 TWD

Reiterate Buy on above-trend growth and margins 
We believe Powertech Technology Inc. (PTI) can continue to benefit from the AI boom 
and an upturn in the memory chip industry. 3Q results clearly confirm record-high 
revenue (NT$25bn; up 27% YoY) and an above-trend gross margin (24%; normal high 
teen% or 20% range). 4Q guidance (for packaging/testing businesses) also remained 
bullish: DRAM – tight supply vs strong demand; NAND – order increase for more value-
added NAND and enterprise SSD; and logic – mix improvement with FC-BGA and price 
hike (following cost increase). Key customers are Micron, Kioxia, Solidigm (owned by SK 
Hynix) and various Greater China logic chip makers that require outsourced packaging 
and testing services. As we have already assumed up-cycle earnings level for 2027-28, 
our EPS estimates remain largely unchanged. We maintain our PO of NT$500 (27x 2027-
28E P/E applied). Reiterate Buy.  
Key takeaways from 3Q results  
Five takeaways from 3Q results: (1) strong execution in newly growing packaging orders 
for high-end memory (low-power / high-speed DRAM, enterprise SSD, 200+ layers 
NAND), (2) increasing exposure to more advanced logic (through FC-BGA, etc), (3) a well-
balanced sales mix (DRAM: 20% of total sales; NAND: 28%; SiP/modules 9%; logic 43%), 
(4) notably improved gross margins (limited increase in manufacturing costs vs strong 
top-line growth seen), and (5) solid FCF generation (EBITDA: c.NT$8bn vs capex: 
NT$5bn; expected annual dividend yield: 3-4% range).

Key Metrics      
* For full definitions of iQmethod SM measures, see page 15.

Company Sector 
Semiconductors

Company Description 
Headquartered in Taiwan, PTI is one of the largest OSAT 
firms, which packages and tests memory and logic chips. It 
has around 18,000 employees. The logic chip-related 
business operates under Greatek Electronics (fully 
consolidated into earnings). Management's new focus areas 
in packaging/testing are PLP, TSV, 3D NAND, low-power 
DRAM, SSD, etc.

Investment Rationale 
Our Buy rating is based on strong earnings growth, driven 
by more value-added memory and even logic packaging 
solutions. 1Q-3Q26 earnings have already hit upcycle levels, 
posting a strong YoY growth (largely driven by NAND/SSD). 
We believe the memory super-cycle and customers' 
ongoing outsourcing trend (Micron, Solidigm/Hynix, Kioxia 
and Greater China's logic chipmakers) using Powertech's 
proven backend solutions should be the key contributors to 
strong earnings growth by 2028.

Exhibit 1: Powertech is accelerating its advanced FOPLP packaging strategy through significant capacity expansion, strategic partnerships (including 
Broadcom), and customer wins such as AMD, positioning itself as a key beneficiary of growing AI semiconductor packaging demand from 2027 onward 
Powertech’s recent news summary during Jul-Aug 2026 
Date Key news Summary 
Aug-2026 FOPLP 
production 
-Powertech plans to start production at the world's first panel-level chip packaging facility for AI chips as soon as early 2027 to make server CPUs, AI 
computing chips and advanced optics 
-Powertech said its advanced FOPLP production line has secured a 100% UTR through 2030 ahead of its scheduled volume productio n in 2027 
-Powertech plans to invest NT$70bn (US$2.2bn) in its FOPLP facility, with installed capacity set to increase from 1,000 panels per months in 2026 
to 5,000 in 2028 
Jul-2026 JV with Broadcom -Powertech reported plans to invest US$400mn to create a new JV with Broadcom in Singapore to cater to customer demand for advanced panel-
based packaging services 
-The JV will concentrate on offering additive finer-geometry redistribution layer (RDL) tech for advanced package substrates  
 AMD business -Powertech reaffirmed that it is on track to start shipping AI chips utilizing its advanced PLP tech for AMD in 2H27, dismissing concern s over delays 
-AMD will be one of Powertech's first customers adopting the FOPLP tech, which is considered to be more cost-effective than the mainstream 
CoWoS tech 
  Capex/capacity -Powertech plans to acquire the entire Philippine unit of ON Semiconductor for US$45mn as part of its efforts to navigate global supply chain 
realignment amid escalating geopolitical risks 
-The transaction will be conducted by its subsidiary, Greatek Electronics in which Powertech holds 43% stake

### Page 13

Price objective basis & risk 
Powertech Technology (PWOGF) 
Our PO of NT$500 is derived from 27x P/E based on 2027-28E EPS. This is 10-20% 
higher than 2024-25 peak levels (low- to mid-20x). Since we expect 50%+ higher EPS in 
2026-28E vs 2024-25 on the back of record-high sales/margin, we believe fair multiples 
should be at least on par with the previous peak level. True, the long-term historical 
average P/E came in the range of 10-20x, but the memory super-cycle and AI boom 
should warrant above-trend earnings growth and consequently over 20x fair P/E. 
Taiwan's OSAT stocks also trade at 20-30x P/E.

Upside risk is mostly related to strong packaging orders from memory chipmakers and 
even logic chip suppliers.

Downside risk is mostly associated with an unexpected memory industry downturn or AI 
bubble (e.g., significant packaging order cut).

年增 22.5%，毛利率則從前期 21.1%降至 20.5%，主要是因客戶

上 ， 投顧預估2026 年 EPS 6.13 元，2027 年 EPS 9.4 元 ， 維持買進 ，
目標價 140 元。

晟銘電 為伺服器機殼大廠，目前
切入 IT 機櫃、水冷機櫃、水冷搭
配的分歧管 
 晟銘電子科技股份有限公司成立於 1976 年 6 月 ， 為台灣伺服器機殼OEM/ODM
製造商， 公司主要銷售電腦機殼及伺服器機殼 ，其用途為桌上型電腦及伺服器等
各項組件之安裝防護設備 ， 近年來公司積極跨足散熱領域並研發設計出液冷散熱
的浸沒式散熱產品。於 2024 年公司推出多款液冷機櫃及液冷散熱系統。晟銘電
提供全球客戶迅速且彈性的 OEM 及 ODM 生產服務。2026 年 1~8 月主要產品
營收占比，伺服器 73%、L11 系統整合 18%、Tooling 6%、DT 3%。

Exhibit 3: 產品組合

Sources : Company、MasterLink

2Q26 本業穩健成長，EPS 1.41
元 
 晟銘電 2Q26 營收 32.19 億元，年增 22.5%，毛利率則從前期 21.1%降至
20.5%，主要是因客戶 Storage 相關產品出貨增加，然 Storage 產品已進入成熟
階段，客戶持續每季議價，2Q26 稅後淨利為 2.9 億元，EPS 1.41 元。

預期 3Q26 為營運谷底，4Q26
開始爬升 
 展望 26H2，3Q26 營收年增 16%，估計 3Q26 稅後 EPS 1.49 元。4Q 通常為
傳統旺季，加上 VR200 也會在 4Q26 陸續加入量產，而晟銘電也有參與其中，
出貨量將逐步上升，隨著 VR200 及 ASIC 等新平台陸續進入放量階段，預期
機殼與 Sidecar 出貨量將同步提升。此外，2026 年 ODM 專案有 8 至 9 成聚焦
機櫃業務，涵蓋 OCP、EIA 以及 MGX，預期 L11 系統整合業務營收將進一步
提升。

今年前八月 L11 系統組裝營收占比已達 18％ ， 而明年L11 系統組裝營收貢獻還
會持續提升。公司在機櫃產品線已相當完整，還可提供配套的水冷配件，機箱
基本上是跟隨著輝達的腳步走 ， 包括GB300、VR200 等標準品機箱都已經開好
模具。海外產能布局，公司目前生產基地仍以中國大陸為主，其中東莞為主要
生產基地，另於寧波設有產能，產品以機殼、機櫃為主，東莞廠自動烤漆線預
計今年底前完成，一天可以完成60 個機櫃烤漆；台灣中壢則主要負責打樣及水
冷產品研發，部分零組件仍由中國生產後運送至中壢進行後段組裝。泰國一期
廠已完成建置並具備生產條件 ， 二期則持續進行設備調校 ， 主要導入烤漆產線，
預計 4Q26 開始試產，後續待客戶完成稽核及認證後逐步量產。泰國二期的烤

年，原專注在車用電子、能源、通訊、電玩

張智皓 chihhao.chang@sinopac.com 
本次報告：法說會

系統電 (5309 TT) 
2027 年重回成長軌道

永豐觀點 
系統電連三個月營收創下新高，預期在 BBU 及無人載具開始出貨下，營收逐月成
長。 
 投資評價與建議 
維持中立投資建議：系統電在經歷營運轉型整合期後，2027 年營收及獲利將重返
成長 ， 然研究員仍維持中立的投資建議 ， 主係考量(1) 營運槓桿效應須待 2H27 時，
BBU 放量出貨較明顯。此外，系統電為因應未來產能持續擴充 BBU 及無人機產線
侵蝕獲利；(2) 評價上，以 2027 年稀釋後稅後 EPS 2.17 元計算，目前本益比約
30 倍，評價尚屬合理，故維持中立的投資建議，持續關注 BBU 出貨及新訂單狀
況。

### Page 2

2

系統電營運主力將轉移至 BBU 相關應用： 系統電子工業股份有限公司成立於1977
年，原專注在車用電子、能源、通訊、電玩遊戲。近年公司陸續進行業務調整及轉
型 ， 將業務分為汽車電子 、 工業物聯網 、AI 資料中心能源系統管理三大事業 ，2015
年推出藍牙胎壓偵測器(BLE TPMS)， 目前主要經營業務內容為車用胎壓偵測系統 、
車用電子產品、綠色能源及儲能設備、電源供應器，並獲有多項專利，並持續擴大
BLE TPMS 應用在歐美 OE 前裝市場，如新能源訂動車、大客車車隊、皮卡等。近
期受到資料中心用電需求增加，系統電 BBU 相關產品將開始出貨。1H26 產品比
重：TPMS 46%、工業電腦 38%、AI 資料中心能源系統及其他 16%。 
3Q26 強固型工業電腦出貨旺，：系統電 9 月營收 3.67 億元(+12.04%MoM，
+17.87%YoY)，連續三個月創下歷史新高，主係因強固型電腦產品出貨。雖 BBU
及無人機相關產品尚未大量出貨，然在工業電腦領域，受惠產業趨勢帶動，如農耕
車及無人載具相關應用成長快速，使系統電 3Q26 營收 10.14 億元(+13%QoQ，
+24%YoY)，研究處預估毛利率 23.2%(+1ppt QoQ，+1.9ppt YoY)，因積極投入
研發及新產品設計使營業費用較高，稅後淨利 0.04 億元，稅後 EPS 為 0.02 元。 
4Q26 兩大專案 BBU 及無人機小量出貨 ：先前耕於已久的 BBU 產品將於十月開始
出貨 ， 初期以3kW 產品為主 ， 系統電已完成建置 2 條全自動 與 1 條半自動 BBU
產線。無人機方面，包含軍用及微型軍警偵察需求強勁，產線亦完成建置。研究處
預期 4Q26 營收 11.17 億元(+10.2%QoQ，+28%YoY)，毛利率 24%(+0.8ppt 
QoQ，-0.6ppt YoY)，稅後淨利 0.07 億元，稅後 EPS 為 0.03 元。預估 2026 年營
收 38.9 億元(+14.7%YoY)，毛利率 23.6%(+0.1pts YoY) ，稅後淨利 0.24 億元(-
54.3%YoY)，稅後 EPS 為 0.11 元。 
2027 年迎來營運收成期：展望 2027 年，以三大事業體區分，工業電腦方面將聚
焦於邊緣算力模組、GCS 地面控制站，部門產品毛利率最佳，有利於獲利表現；AI
資料中心能源系統方面，BBU 產品將開始大量出貨，並持續擴充產能，預估部門
營收將年增 328%，並將增加 3-4 條 BBU 產線，因應未來新產品的加入；TPMS
業務收到車市影響，營運相對平淡。綜上所述，系統電2027 年營運將開始收成過
去在無人載具及 BBU 電源產品的投入，研究處預期 2027 年營收 61.68 億元
(+58.5%YoY)，毛利率 24.9%(+1.3pts YoY) ，稅後淨利 5.41 億元，稅後 EPS 為
2.36 元。

營運現況與分析

水冷與 MI450 接棒，27 年營收亮眼

營收走勢圖

研究員/莊佳蓉 EVA8894122@uni-psg.com

研究部對緯穎(6669)維持買進建議，主係：(1) 3Q26營收季增56%，
大幅優於預期；(2) 4Q26在AWS的ASIC氣冷出貨帶動下，預期4Q營
收季增個位數 ；(3) 雖T3水冷機櫃短期遞延， 但預估27年逐月攀升，
公司仍為主要供應商。(4) AMD MI450 於1H27年放量，客戶與平台
更加多元；(5) 新增之Oracle VR200預期的訂單約2.5K，約在27年
貢獻營收。 綜上所述，研究部上修 2026/2027 年營收至1.46兆元
/3.07兆元(原估1.27兆元/2.38兆元)，EPS上修至143.16元/241.16
元，目前評價僅8~9倍，位於低檔，給予12.5倍本益比，目標價3010
元(2027EPS*12.5PER)。

重點摘要： 
1. 3Q26營收大幅優於預期 
緯穎9月營收為1,726億元，月增19.6%，年增100%，整體3Q26
營收達4,346億元 ，季增56.2%，年增63%，營收達成率達到
135%(研究部預估)/128%(市場共識)， 大幅優於預期 ， 主係Meta
通用型及AWS T3氣冷出貨旺盛所致。3Q26財報尚未公布，研究
部上修至毛利率8.9%(原估8.1%)， 季減0.4pts， 優於原先預期 ，
主係通用伺服器占比較高， 產品組合較佳 ，對毛利率形成支撐，
預估3Q EPS約45.56元。

2. 4Q營運動能可望延續 
展望4Q26，雖水冷機櫃須遞延至年底至1H27才會逐漸放量 ，但
仍預期T3氣冷機櫃及通用持續出貨，預估4Q26營收約4,666億
元，季增7.4%，年增59.5%。毛利率8.2% (原估7.3%)，季減
0.7pts， 主係高單價ASIC水冷機櫃占比尚低， 預估EPS約45.18
元。整體而言，2H26在ASIC氣冷及通用伺服器帶動下，營運明
顯優於上半年

3. T3短期雜音不改長期趨勢，2027年水冷逐步放量 
根據供應鏈調查：(1) T3水冷機櫃初期產線磨合不順，客戶因
此下修今年出貨規劃。不過，2027年水冷機櫃出貨規劃仍逐季
攀升，在晶片端的水冷比重也提升 至50~70%，單價為氣冷3-4
倍以上，成為2027年營收成長的主要動能。在AWS下一代T4產
品，公司持續參與機櫃設計，有望採用全水冷設計，預期28年
出貨，為中長期成長動能。

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

(2)MI450機櫃部分，Meta專案集中在2027年出貨，單價約450
～500萬美元，訂單逐漸明確，預估約可達5～7K櫃。(3) 新增
Oracle VR200訂單，預期約2.5K櫃，約在27年開始貢獻營收。
公司在GPU伺服器的客戶版圖進一步擴大。

4. 目前評價位於低檔，維持買進建議 
綜上所述，考量各客戶訂單逐漸明確， 研究部上修2026/2027
年營收至1.46兆元/3.07兆元(原估1.27兆元/2.38兆元)，2027
年毛利率雖然因高單價水冷機櫃及GPU機櫃比重提升，下滑至
6.5%(原估7.1%)， 但營收規模大幅擴張 ， 獲利仍可望顯著成長 。
股本部分，緯穎配發股票股利，除權基準日為9/8，配股率達
1.9828倍，股本由18.69億元增至56.02億元，EPS因此依比例
稀釋，故2026/2027年稀釋後EPS上修至143.16元/241.16元。
目前評價僅8~9倍，位於低檔，給予12倍本益比，目標價3010
元(2027EPS*12.5PER)。

附錄 一 ： 評 等 之 標 準 
評等  定義  
強力買進     預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬超過 25%以上  
買進  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬 10% 以上  
中立  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於 10～-10%  
降低持股  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於 (-10%)以下

告 中 所 有 的 意 見 及 預 估 ， 如 有 變 更 恕 不 另 行 通 知 。

本 研 究 報 告 所 載 之 投 資 資 訊 ， 僅 提 供 客 戶 做 為 一 般 投 資 參 考 ， 並 非 針 對 特 定 對 象 提
供 專 屬 之 投 資 建 議 。 文 中 所 載 資 訊 或 任 何 意 見 ， 不 構 成 任 何 買 賣 有 價 證 券 或 其 他 投
資 標 的 之 要 約 、 宣 傳 或 引 誘 等 事 項 。 對 於 本 投 資 報 告 所 討 論 或 建 議 之 任 何 證 券 、 投
資 標 的 ， 或 文 中 所 討 論 或 建 議 之 投 資 策 略 ， 投 資 人 應 就 其 是 否 適 合 本 身 財 務 狀 況 與
投 資 條 件 ， 進 一 步 諮 詢 財 務 顧 問 的 意 見 。 本 投 資 報 告 之 內 容 取 材 自 據 信 為 可 靠 之 資
料 來 源 ， 但 概 不 以 明 示 或 默 示 的 方 式 ， 對 資 料 之 準 確 性 、 完 整 性 或 正 確 性 作 出 任 何
陳 述 或 保 證 。 本 投 資 報 告 載 述 意 見 進 行 更 改 與 撤 回 並 不 另 行 通 知 。 本 投 資 報 告 並 非
（且不應 解 釋 為 ）在 任 何 司 法 管 轄 區 內 ， 任 何 非 依 法 從 事 證 券 經 紀 或 交 易 之 人 士 或
公 司 ， 為 於 該 管 轄 區 內 從 事 證 券 經 紀 或 交 易 之 遊 說 。 本 投 資 報 告 內 容 屬 統 一 投 顧 之
著 作 權 ， 嚴 禁 抄 襲 與 仿 造 。

## Document 233: Advanced economies _ Resilience to shock after shock (2026-09-24)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Advanced economies  _ Resilience to shock after shock (2026-09-24).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-09-24
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

3
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoPerspectives / 3rd Quarter 2026 / September 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
History tends to repeat itself in advanced economies. Once again, growth ultimately fell short of expectations by only a small
margin in the first half of 2026, despite the conflict in Iran. As early as 2025, the impact of tariffs was less severe than feared.
This is a sign that structural factors (notably AI, defence and electrification) are underpinning growth in these countries

and are expected to continue to do so in 2027. However, there are significant risks to the economic outlook, primarily of a
geopolitical nature, with a significant upside risk to inflation and interest rates. The other instance of history repeating itself
concerns a period that those under 20 cannot have experienced: the period leading up to the 2008 crisis, echoes of which can
be seen in terms of growth, inflation, the direction of monetary policy, and long-term interest rates.

Advanced economies are experiencing trends similar to those they
faced nearly 20 years ago:
- Growth remains resilient in 2025-26 despite shocks, as it did in 2006-
07, in the major advanced economies.
- Inflation is above central banks’ targets: as in 2006-07, this calls for a
tightening of monetary policy.
- In Europe and the United States, long-term interest rates have re -
turned to their 2006-07 levels (1996 in Japan): a challenging environ-

ment for public finances in a context where public debt ratios are signi-
ficantly higher than those seen 20 years ago.
THE GLOBAL ECONOMY IN THE MIDST OF A GROWTH CYCLE
We are already well into 2026, and the picture so far is clear: glo -
bal growth is proving resilient. Following a US tariff shock last year,
growth is once again proving, for the time being, to be significantly less
affected by the conflict in Iran than had been anticipated when it first

broke out. Initially, however, there were fears that this new shock would
hit growth even harder. As with the tariff hike in 2025, the impact was
measurable, particularly in terms of consumer confidence. But in both
2025 and 2026, growth has also shown a degree of resilience.
The figures for the second quarter bear this out, despite the sharp rise
in oil prices, the recessionary impact of which is not yet apparent.
According to our forecasts, growth was expected to reach or exceed 1%

in 2026 in the four major advanced economies (the Eurozone, Japan,
the United Kingdom and the United States, where it exceeds 2%) for
the second consecutive year. This had not happened (excluding the
post-COVID catch-up period) since 2006-07. Growth figures are more
moderate (particularly for demographic reasons), but the private sec -
tor is not (at present) showing the same excesses (at that time, the
credit boom had preceded the worst crisis since the 1930s); although

debt levels have recently risen in the US tech sector.
The driving force behind the current growth cycle is clear: artificial
intelligence (AI). It is fuelling global trade and business investment
in most countries. This is clearly the case in the United States, where
business investment is growing strongly as investment plans in the
US tech sector are implemented.
However, the world’s major economies are currently engaged in a
broader push to expand their capital goods investment. Alongside the

ongoing development of services—particularly digital services—we
have seen a rebound in industrial production in most countries since
early 2025. The leading sectors are primarily technology equipment,
machinery, aeronautics, defence and their essential inputs (energy and
metals). This trend is evident everywhere, including in Europe, and
is becoming entrenched over the long term, with an improvement in
business climate indicators, particularly new export orders. Growth is

therefore expected to strengthen further in 2027, particularly in the
Eurozone, where German investment plans are set to gather momen -
tum and whose spillover effects will have a greater impact on the eco-
nomy.
However, not all sectors are experiencing the same momentum. This
is particularly true for the sectors that depend on household consump-
tion, which has shown mixed trends. Our observation that consumer
spending is more buoyant in the United States than in Europe was

confirmed in 2026, and the outlook does not point to a change in this
ranking.
EDITORIAL
RESILIENCE TO SHOCK AFTER SHOCK

### Page 2

EcoPerspectives / 3rd Quarter 2026 / September 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
The bank
for a changing
world
While European and US households are experiencing more or less the
same energy price shock, inflation remains higher in the United States.
But this has not prevented consumer spending from keeping pace, pri-
marily because it is driven by the wealthiest households, who are less
sensitive to inflation than to the wealth effect. Furthermore, while the

savings rate remains high in the Eurozone, US households have been
drawing down their savings. This is also true of British households,
which explains why the UK has seen slightly stronger growth since
the beginning of the year. In Japan, government spending has partially
offset energy inflation (while other countries are doing so to a much
lesser extent than in 2022), which has supported consumption growth.
However, these buffers—reduced savings or fiscal support—have their

limits when shocks persist. The two major current conflicts (Iran and
Ukraine) are far from being resolved, and their impact is continuing.
This persistent geopolitical risk and its implications for inflation repre-
sent a significant headwind to the growth outlook for 2027.
HIGHER INFLATION CALLS FOR MONETARY TIGHTENING
Inflation is exceeding the target set by three of the four central banks
examined in this publication, which is another similarity (albeit with

different underlying causes) with the 2006-07 period. Japan is the ex -
ception, as government support measures for households are tempo -
rarily holding back inflation.
Apart from the Bank of Japan, which is engaged in a long-term norma-
lisation process, no central bank was expected to raise its key rates
in 2026. But they are now being prompted to do so for reasons that
vary from one region to another. The first is the conflict in Iran and
the resulting rise in oil prices, which triggered a rebound in energy

inflation from the second quarter onwards. However, since the conflict
was not resolved by the memorandum of understanding signed in June,
and given that the subsequent drop in oil prices proved short-lived,
inflation is expected to rise again. The rise in gas prices is expected to
exacerbate the impact, particularly in Europe, where inventories are
being replenished ahead of winter. Electricity prices are expected to
follow suit in most countries, but at a more moderate pace than in

2022. Indeed, gas is now used less to generate electricity than it was
at that time (thanks to the expansion of renewable energy), and to an
even lesser extent in France.
There is a significant risk that energy inflation will spread further to
other components of inflation in the future, and inflation expectations
could eventually reflect this. This is why the European Central Bank
has already raised its key interest rate twice (in June and September)

and, according to our scenario, will likely do so again in December. For
the same reason, we expect the Bank of England to raise its policy rate
in November. Although the cap on energy prices was maintained for
a long time, it has now been raised. As a result, inflation is expected
to rise between now and the end of the year. The aim will then be to
prevent second-round effects.
Inflation can also be a sign of (too much) economic strength. In the

United States, it has exceeded the 2% target since 2021. The main
argument in favour of a new cycle of rate hikes (following the one in
2022-2023) lies in the current resilience of growth and the improving
labour market. The Federal Reserve (Fed) must balance two equally
important objectives: maximum employment and price stability. Howe-
ver, unlike in the 4 th quarter of 2025, when the decline in job crea -
tion justified monetary easing (by 75 basis points), the current sharp

rebound in job creation completely reverses that analysis. According
to our forecasts, the unemployment rate is expected to decrease in
the coming months, which pushes back the prospect of a decline in
core inflation. This situation persists due to both supply constraints
(over which a central bank has no control) and excess demand (which
the Fed can, however, mitigate). Consequently, the Fed raised its key
rate in September and is expected to implement two further rate hikes

between now and January 2027.
In Japan, finally, while headline inflation has been brought down by
government support measures, core inflation remains significant.
Wage pressures are mounting against a backdrop of labour shortages.
The Bank of Japan is therefore being forced to accelerate the pace of its
monetary normalisation.
In all these regions, inflation is currently expected to peak in Q4 2026
or Q1 2027. It is against this backdrop of temporary inflation that rate

hikes could also come to an end by that time (excluding Japan). Howe-
ver, this assumes that once the energy shock—the course of which
remains uncertain—subsides, inflation will fall. The effects of El Niño,
which are still difficult to gauge at present, could take over. Inflation
would then remain above the central banks’ target for a longer period.
Under this scenario, rate hikes would go further than is currently en -
visaged.
THE RISE IN LONG- TERM INTEREST RATES: A FACTOR THAT WILL WEIGH

ON PUBLIC FINANCES
Monetary policies have also to offset the inflationary effects of fiscal
policy, particularly in the United States (where the budget deficit is
high and shows no signs of shrinking) and in Japan (where the new
government is pursuing pro-growth policies). In Europe, fiscal policy
is playing a much smaller role than it did in 2022. While the stimulus
measures implemented at that time did help reduce the inflation rate
in the short term (by nearly 2.5 pp in France, for example), by keeping

demand at a high level, they delayed disinflation and increased the
scale of the monetary tightening required.
The outbreak of the conflict in Iran acted as a catalyst: it triggered a
rise in long-term rates (which had been trending downwards at the
start of the year) to levels close to those of 2006-07. This rise is wides-
pread, but its causes vary. Inflation expectations appear to play a more
significant role in the Eurozone, whereas the rise in real interest rates

seems to be the main factor in the United States and Japan.

The bank
for a changing
world
Higher inflation and more resilient real growth than feared point in
the same direction: stronger than expected nominal growth that is li -
kely to be sustained. However, in the long term, high nominal growth
tends to drive up nominal interest rates (as it is a positive sign of more
profitable investment projects), in contrast to the problem of secular

stagnation raised in the 2010s (which combined low nominal growth
with low nominal interest rates).
The other issue is the high level of fiscal deficits (too high for a period
of growth), with various situations. In several countries, the public
debt-to-GDP ratio continues to rise. In the United States, deficit reduc-
tion does not appear to be a priority, to the extent that it is expected
to remain above 6% of GDP at the very least until the end of Donald

Trump’s term of office (with little hope that the mid-term elections will
significantly alter this trend). In the United Kingdom and France, fiscal
consolidation has begun, but it is being hampered by the simultaneous
increase in certain areas of expenditure (debt servicing, defence and
healthcare, due in particular to an ageing population), which is slowing
the pace of consolidation. In Germany, the implementation of invest -
ment plans is leading to a widening of the deficit. While this does not

pose a sustainability problem, it does result in an increase in annual
debt issuance. In Japan, the budget deficit is kept in check by interest
payments that remain low, as the rise in interest rates is recent and
the average maturity of the debt is high (10 years). However, expansio-
nary fiscal policy could pose further problems in terms of debt dyna -
mics if it were to continue through the end of the decade. In Italy and
Spain, fiscal consolidation has been sufficient to stabilise the public

debt-to-GDP ratio (and even to reduce it in Spain), but the high level of
Italy’s debt service it vulnerable to a rise in interest rates.
In all these countries, the fiscal arithmetic is simpler today than it
will be in 2030. Indeed, in most cases, only half of the debt was is -
sued after 2022 (that is, after the end of the zero-interest-rate period).
The debt service has so far only partially risen, except in the United
States, where most of the increase has already occurred due to a shor-

ter average debt maturity (6 years). This means that, for all countries
combined, the rate of GDP growth remains higher than the average
interest rate paid on public debt (or the effective interest rate).
By 2030, the effective rate will have risen and caught up with
GDP growth. As a result, in most cases, the debt service will have re -
turned to the level as a share of GDP seen in the early 2000s. In order to
stabilise public debt-to-GDP ratios, primary balances will need to have

improved. This will even require the generation of primary surpluses,
particularly in Italy (which is already achieving this), France and the
United Kingdom. This is a crucial shift that governments will need to
introduce as early as the 2027 budget; otherwise, they risk postponing
the stabilisation of their public debt-to-GDP ratio once again.
Article completed on 17 September 2026
Stéphane Colliac
stephane.colliac@bnpparibas.com

## Document 253: Bank of Canada likely to hike again after today’s move

Source PDF: all report pdf/Bank of Canada likely to hike again after today’s move.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-28

### Page 1

The BoC raised the policy rate by 25bp to 2.5% today, matching
market expectations. Policymakers acknowledge rising
inﬂationary risks, and while not recommitting to a rate path,
the indications are that this won’t be a one-and-done hike. We
expect the next move in 1Q27, with risks of an earlier one in
December. We remain cautious on CAD's upside risks

James Knightley and Francesco Pesole
The BoC raised rates to 2.5% today
BoC to proceed carefully after hike
The Bank of Canada has raised its target for the overnight rate by
25bp to 2.5%. Ahead of the meeting, it was about 80% priced by
markets, with economists split down the middle on whether we
would get a hike or not – the Canadian outlook is improving, but
inﬂation is in line with target and the recent jobs numbers have
been disappointing with the unemployment rate rising to 6.5%.
The statement justiﬁes the action on the basis that the recent
growth ﬁgures have been stronger than anticipated thanks in part

In an environment of rising cost pressures, which so far have been

increase the policy rate further..." but they will "proceed carefully".
Market expectations still too hawkish
Markets are currently seeing the next meeting in December as a
50:50 call on whether we get another hike. Right now our forecast is
for the BoC to hike rates to 2.75% in the ﬁrst quarter of 2027 (our
preference is January over March) and hold them steady, but the
risk is that they go a little more aggressively, and we see a

December hike followed by a 1Q27 move.
Anyway, we still judge market expectations for 90bp of tightening
over the next 12 months as overly hawkish. The BoC has a central
estimate for the neutral rate around 2.75%, and we agree with their
estimate that second-round eﬀects will be present but overall
contained, especially in terms of wage growth spillover.

In the near term, elevated oil prices risk prompting an upward

could lead to broad dovish repricing and release pressure on the
BoC to hike in December.

## Document 356: Persistent energy pressures have further to run (2026-10-28)

Key points
• Shipping disruptions, depleted
inventories and elevated freight
costs are keeping physical crude
and fuel markets tight despite a

reported recovery in Gulf exports.
• Brent is forecast to average

to US$77/bbl in Q4 2027.

are based are reasonable, the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
Low inventories and higher shipping costs will keep oil and
fuel prices elevated, with Brent futures forecast to average
US$101/bbl in Q4 before easing to US$77/bbl by Q4 2027
as supply recovers. Refined fuels and LNG prices should

ease more slowly. Risks are two-sided: escalation could
push Brent towards US$150/bbl, while an early US-Iran
agreement could see a faster normalisation in supply with
prices returning close to pre-conflict levels by end-2027.
This article was first published in the October Market Outlook .
Brent crude is back above US$100/bbl on fading confidence
in a lasting US-Iran agreement and intermittent attacks on
shipping. Buffers remain depleted, with the IEA estimating

global observed inventories were 507mb lower at the end of
August than at the start of the conflict.
This comes despite reported crude exports through the Strait of
Hormuz recovering to around 70% to 90% of pre-conflict levels
in late September. Saudi and UAE exports through the Yanbu and
Fujairah pipelines continue, with the East-West pipeline back to
normal after the early September attacks. Tanker crossings have
risen, although transponders remain switched off, leaving visible

transit at 1% to 4% of pre-conflict levels, while cargo transfers in
the Gulf of Oman have increased as an alternative route.
However, these workarounds are slower and more costly. Ship-
to-ship transfers can take 5 to 10 days, US escorts through
the Gulf via a route close to the Oman shoreline cause delays
while some tankers are taking longer routes. Fewer ships are
also available, with many redeployed to other markets. Higher
insurance premiums are adding to costs, with Gulf-to-China

tanker rates above US$0.9mn a day, around 15x higher than a
year ago. With prompt barrels (available for physical delivery)
scarce, the gap between physical and futures prices widened
again, with Dated Brent around US$136/bbl in early October
against US$101/bbl for front-month futures.
Oil and fuel prices push higher ...
Refined prices have risen more than crude
Refined product markets were already tight going into the
conflict. Reduced Gulf supply, exacerbated by Ukrainian strikes

on Russian refineries, has added to that tightness. Together
with higher shipping costs and rising Dated Brent prices,
Singapore Gasoil 10ppm (diesel) has roughly doubled since the
Strait closed, compared with around 40% for crude futures.
Consequently, Singapore gasoil crack spread widened
to an average of around US$36.1/bbl in early October,
from US$17.5/bbl across 2025. Alongside the Australian
government’s excise tax returning to normal, after it had been

halved, this has lifted national diesel and petrol prices to
around A$2.83/L and A$2.41/L.
Outlook
We do not see a lasting US-Iran agreement until at least
mid-2027. In the meantime, we assume there will not be a
return to full-scale military intervention. However, periodic
Iranian attacks on ships and facilities are expected to continue,
as will the US blockade of Iranian exports, keeping a supply
risk premium in place. We look for Brent futures to average

around US$101/bbl in Q4, then ease gradually as demand
from refiners and governments to rebuild stocks partly offsets
recovering supply. As the premium fades and the market
moves into surplus in late 2027, we forecast Brent at around
US$77/bbl in Q4 2027, easing towards US$70/bbl in 2028 as
Middle East production fully recovers.
Oil balance to gradually improve over 2027
Global oil supply is expected to rise by 4.0mb/d (4.0%) to
103.7mb/d in 2027, following a 6.1% fall in 2026. Most of

this reflects a gradual recovery in Gulf output, with Houthi

to 105.6mb/d in 2027. Chinese crude imports will continue to
recover over the coming months as refiners restock ahead of
Lunar New Year, but we expect only a gradual return to pre-

conflict levels, as the electrification of transport limits the
recovery in Chinese demand. Globally, demand for diesel and jet
fuel recovers as transport and aviation activity picks up, although
some demand destruction and efficiency gains are expected
to be permanent. The conflict has also highlighted strategic
vulnerabilities, particularly across Asia, so we expect a push to
rebuild reserves adding around 0.5% to demand over 2027.
Fuel prices to ease more slowly than crude

Refined product supply is expected to recover more slowly
than crude, with Gulf refinery runs not returning to pre-war
levels until at least late 2027. Outside the Gulf, there has
been some constructive news. The US has lifted sanctions
on Russian diesel until April 2027, with Russia saying it will
supply more than 4Mt over the coming months, while China
has lifted its ban on refined product exports. However, we
are cautious about how much this will add to the market in

the short term. Much of the Russian supply is headed to the
US and will depend on Russia’s ability to restore refining and
export capacity, while doubts remain over the Russia-Ukraine
energy ceasefire announced by President Trump. China still
manages exports through quotas, which are likely to increase
only gradually as domestic stocks are rebuilt. Refinery runs
should lift ahead of Lunar New Year, but any increase in
exports will take time to reach regional markets.

With product supply recovering slowly and depleted inventories
being rebuilt, Singapore gasoil crack is likely to stay above its
long-run average even as Brent eases, narrowing from US$71/
bbl in Q4 to US$27/bbl by Q4 2027. With Singapore Gas 10ppm
prices, shipping and insurance costs to remain elevated and
easing only gradually in 2027, we forecast Australian diesel
to retail at an average of 274¢/L in Q4, easing to 200¢/L in Q4
2027, and petrol at 230¢/L and 187¢/L respectively.

LNG prices supported through 2027
Japanese LNG prices surged 15.2%mth in September,
averaging US$25.4/MMBtu as supply disruptions in Qatar
and its inability to divert exports around the Strait of Hormuz
tightened markets and intensified competition between
importers. While prices are now more than double pre-conflict
levels, demand destruction across Asia is providing some
offset to tight supply
Our Q4 forecast for Japanese LNG is now US$24.5/mmbtu and

we expect the market to remain structurally tight, with supply
recovery likely to be gradual and prices not returning to pre-
conflict levels until around 2028. Still, Australia’s domestic
gas market should remain insulated, supported by the FY2027
Federal Budget’s domestic gas reservation scheme, which
requires exporters to supply the domestic market with the
equivalent of up to 20% of gas exports. So far this year,
despite the increase in LNG spot prices in Asia, Australian

natural gas prices have remained under the $12 per GJ cap
that has proposed by the Commonwealth government. We are
expecting this to continue well into 2027.
Risks around our oil price outlook are two-sided
Talks are still ongoing in the background, with the US and
Iran exchanging proposals to reopen the Strait, but the gap
between them remains wide. The conflict has also expanded
to the Red Sea and, with inventories sharply drawn down,
there is a smaller buffer to absorb a further shock.

Using the Oxford Economics model, we assess an escalation
scenario, where energy markets face a deeper and more
persistent disruption as the conflict in the Middle East
intensifies. In this case, Brent futures rise to around US$150/
bbl in H1 2027, only dipping to below US$100/bbl in Q2 2028.
We also consider an early resolution scenario, where shipping
and Gulf supply normalise faster, returning Brent to near pre-
conflict levels by end-2027.
Escalation scenario

In the escalation scenario we assume:
Talks stall and the conflict escalates:  the gap between the
US and Iran proves too wide and the US resumes strikes after
the midterms. Iran responds with attacks on shipping and Gulf
energy infrastructure, damaging export, storage and refining
facilities. A lasting agreement is not reached until 2028.
Export routes are disrupted: more frequent attacks curtail
dark crossings of the Strait and ship-to-ship transfers in the

Gulf of Oman. Houthi attacks also cut off Saudi alternative
routes, forcing Gulf producers to cut output and Middle
East crude exports sharply lower. New routes and shipping
arrangements eventually support a recovery in Gulf flows, but
trade patterns take time to adjust.
... and risks remain and while they are two sided ...

are based are reasonable, the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
Product supply fails to recover:  The Russia–Ukraine energy
ceasefire does not hold and strikes on Russian refineries
continue, limiting how much diesel Russia can export,
even with the US sanctions waiver extended. China also

reintroduces its export ban to protect domestic supply.
Oil prices lift to around US$150/bbl in H1 2027: With little
buffer to absorb the supply loss, competition for physical
delivery intensifies, with physical prices well above futures
as prompt barrels remain scarce. Higher prices support
supply growth outside OPEC and drive demand destruction,
alongside further emergency stock releases, but a deeper
and more prolonged global deficit remains. Brent stays above

US$100/bbl until mid-2028 as governments rebuild reserves.
The Singapore gasoil crack widens and, with freight and
insurance costs also higher, Australian diesel and petrol prices
average around 247 cpl and 231 cpl at end 2027. Japanese
LNG also remains elevated around US$23.2/MMBtu in Q4
2027, $9.1 above our baseline.
Early resolution scenario
In the early resolution scenario we assume:
A durable agreement: The US and Iran agree terms to reopen
the Strait by the end of year, and, unlike the June interim deal,

the agreement holds, with progress towards a permanent
settlement in early 2027. Attacks on shipping cease and the
US blockade is lifted, allowing Iranian oil back into the market,
initially from storage. Vessels are quickly repositioned to the
Gulf, with freight and war-risk insurance easing from early
2027 rather than mid-year.
A faster recovery in supply:  With export routes secure, Gulf
production and damaged refinery and export capacity recover

sooner than in the baseline. Outside the Gulf, the US extends
its waiver on Russian diesel beyond April 2027 and China
raises export quotas as domestic stocks are rebuilt.
Demand settles below pre-conflict trend: Some demand
destruction and efficiency gains persist, particularly in China.
With inventories rebuilding faster, government restocking is
smaller and more gradual than in the baseline.
Prices fall quickly, but restocking provides a floor:  Brent

Brent futures ease to around US$88/bbl in Q1 2027 as the
premium for prompt physical barrels unwinds. As the market
moves into balance, Brent eases to around US$65/bbl by Q4
2027, close to pre-conflict levels.
Refined product prices ease faster on lower freight costs with
the Singapore gasoil crack narrowing. Australian diesel and
petrol prices fall to around 182 cpl and 170 cpl by Q4 2027. Gas
markets also ease, although damage to Qatar’s gas facility means

export capacity recovers gradually. Japanese LNG falls to around
US$11.9/MMBtu by end-2027, $2.1 below our baseline.
Economic impact
In the escalation scenario, higher fuel costs flow through to
transport, food and other energy-intensive goods such as
plastics and fertilisers. Australian headline CPI remains above
4.5% for much of 2027, with underlying inflation remaining
sticky at above 3% until late 2028. The RBA faces a difficult
trade off between much higher inflation and a significant

hit to activity, as households cut discretionary spending and
businesses defer investment. Higher LNG export earnings
provide only a moderate offset. In the early resolution scenario,
headline inflation is around 0.1ppt below the baseline in 2027.
New Zealand is more exposed in both scenarios, with food
and energy a larger share of its CPI and no offsetting energy
exports. Headline inflation is around 1ppt higher in the
escalation scenario in Q1 2027.

... the upside presents a more dramatic shift in prices

## Document 373: September CPI_ Inflation accelerating in the run up to Christmas (2026-10-28)

Source PDF: all report pdf/September CPI_ Inflation accelerating in the run up to Christmas (2026-10-28).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-28
Pages: 18
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#inflation #australia #rba #economicbulletinsaustralia #inflation
September CPI: Inflation accelerating in the run up to Christmas
By Justin Smirk & Neha Sharma
16:08 October 28 2026 Share
September Monthly CPI: Headline 0.8%mth/4.3%yr, Trimmed Mean 0.3.%mth/3.6%yr.
Market services ex volatile 1.6%mth/4.0%yr. September Quarter CPI: Headline
1.3%qtr/3.9%yr; Trimmed Mean 1.0%qtr/3.7%yr.
The CPI rose 0.8% in the September month, stronger than Westpac’s 0.6% estimate and the

market’s expectation of 0.7%. The annual pace picked up from 4.0%yr to 4.3%yr, The seasonally
adjusted CPI rose 0.6% in September highlighting some positive seasonality in the month.

### Page 2

Key factors driving the headline monthly result were: automotive fuels (9.4%mth); domestic holiday
travel & accommodation (8.0%mth); international holiday travel & accommodation (7.2%mth); and
property rates & charges (5.5%mth). There were modest offsets from: women’s garments (–
3.1%mth); household textiles (–6.6%mth); and accessories (–4.1%mth).
The Trimmed Mean (TM) gained 0.3% in September, spot on both Westpac and market expectations
with the annual pace lifting from 3.5%yr (revised from 3.6%yr) to 3.6%yr. The six-month annualised

pace is now 4.0%yr the fastest in the short history of this series.
Headline CPI lifted 1.3% in the September quarter compared to an expectation of 1.3% by Westpac
and 1.2% by the market. This saw the annual pace hold at 3.9%yr.
The RBA’s key underlying inflation measure, the quarterly TM, lifted 1.0% vs an expectation of
0.9% by Westpac and market estimate of 1.0%. The annual pace lifted from 3.6%yr to 3.7%yr
with the six-month annualised pace accelerating to 3.7%yr from 3.4%yr.

Overall, the estimates come in broadly as expected so we see minimal risk to our current December
quarter forecast of 1.3%qtr for the headline CPI. Softer housing inflation does present a very modest
downside risk to our 0.9%qtr for the TM but there are offsetting gains elsewhere.
The September CPI update revealed underlying inflationary pressures continuing even while a
moderation in housing inflation (dwellings in particular) is unfolding slightly faster than we had

anticipated. As such, the near-term risk for inflation remains to the upside. The RBA will remain alert
to these near-term inflationary risks and as so, we hold to the view that the RBA will raise rates again
at the November meeting.

### Page 3

Month of September CPI
The monthly CPI rose 0.8%mth in September, above both our expectation of a 0.6%mth increase and
the market forecast of 0.7%mth. This lifted the annual pace by 0.3ppts to 4.3%yr. On a seasonally
adjusted basis, the CPI increased 0.6%mth.
The key contributors to the monthly increase were: automotive fuel (9.4%mth, +0.3ppts); and holiday
travel & accommodation (7.6%mth, +0.5ppts), alongside a smaller contribution from the annual reset in

property rates (5.5%mth, +0.1ppt). There was no single major offset. Instead, several components
across household furnishings and clothing & footwear recorded modest declines, together subtracting a
little over 0.1ppt from the monthly result.
The monthly trimmed mean (TM) rose 0.3%mth, in line with both our and the market’s expectations.
The annual pace edged up to 3.6%yr, although the August outcome was revised down to 3.5%yr.
Underlying inflation has remained broadly stuck in the 3.5–3.6%yr range since May. On a 3m/3m basis,

the monthly TM was 1.1%.

### Page 4

Market services excluding volatile items and holiday travel were flat in the month, with annual inflation
easing to 3.8%yr, its softest pace since November 2025. On a seasonally adjusted basis, monthly
inflation moderated across most components in this measure, with telecommunications services, rents
and other financial services the key exceptions. However, all but two components are still recording
annual price growth above 3%, suggesting that disinflation across market services remains slow and

relatively narrow. The persistence of these underlying price pressures is likely to remain a concern for
the RBA.

### Page 5

Housing inflation was notably softer in the month, rising 0.3%mth to be 5.2% higher over the year.
Rents increased 0.2%mth, slightly below our expectation for a 0.3%mth rise. The biannual indexation
of Commonwealth Rent Assistance occurred late in the month, weighing on the September outcome,
with the remainder of the impact expected to flow through in October. The ABS noted that rents would
have risen 0.3%mth in the absence of the measure, while on a seasonally adjusted basis rents

increased 0.4%mth.
New dwelling inflation was flat in the month, the softest reading since May 2025. Monthly inflation has
now slowed in each successive month since May 2026, suggesting homebuilders’ pricing power is
becoming increasingly constrained. The ABS noted that some project home builders raised base prices
this month only to be offset by some builders increasing discounts and promotional offers in response
to slowing demand. The annual pace eased to 4.7%yr. Dwelling repair costs were also a downside

surprise, falling 0.1%mth, their first decline since February 2025, although prices remain 3.4% higher
over the year. Some of this softness was offset by a firmer-than-expected increase in property rates
and charges, which rose 5.6%mth.
Recently, we have also focused on audio, visual and computing given the potential for spillovers from
strong global demand for semiconductors and other computing resources associated with the AI build
out. Prices for both equipment and services came in weaker than August, with equipment prices falling

1.1%mth, while services rose 0.3%mth. Despite this, annual price growth remains relatively high for
these categories, running at 4.6% and 4.9% respectively. Some of this pressure might also be showing

### Page 6

through in gaming, toys and hobbies, which is running at 5.2%mth/15.5%yr.
Overall, the breadth of inflation moderated for a second consecutive month. The share of the basket
running above 3%yr fell from 40% to 35%, meanwhile the share below 2%yr lifted from 27% to 32%.
Our replication of the San Francisco Fed’s inflation shock momentum served as a good guide to the
quarterly TM. With the full monthly reads in for the September quarter in, this measure does signal
some persistence/upside pressures for the quarterly TM going into the coming quarters. It is worth
cautioning that this measure will be impacted as the ABS refines its seasonal adjustment process for
the monthly indexes.

Compared to our expectations, the key surprises were:
Upside surprises
Downside surprises
Fruit & vegetables was the key upside surprise, 0.6%mth against our expectations for 3.7%mth fall.
Holiday travel & accommodation also surprised to the upside, following a run of downside surprises
since early this year. It rose 7.6%mth against our expectations for a 4.4%mth lift. 
Automotive fuel rose 9.4%mth against expectations of an 8.3%mth lift.

### Page 7

September Quarter CPI
Clothing & footwear was the key downside surprise with prices falling 2.7%mth against our
expectations for a 0.2%mth fall. The misses were broad-based: garments (–2.3%mth vs –2.0%mth);
footwear (–2.5%mth vs 1.8%mth); and accessories & clothing services (–4.1%mth vs 2.8%mth).
Household textiles declined 6.6%mth against our expectations for a 3.2%mth fall. Furniture &
furnishings rose by less-than-expected 0.4%mth vs 1.6%mth. Household appliances, utensils and

tools also came in softer, –3.2%mth vs –2.4%mth.
Dairy prices fell 1.2%mth against our expectations for a 0.1%mth lift.

### Page 8

The September quarter CPI printed spot on our expectation of 1.3%. The key factor driving this
increase was the slightly stronger surge in auto fuel (7.8%, +0.27ppt) and holiday travel &
accommodation (2.7%qtr, +0.16ppt) being offset by a slightly softer than expected increase in housing
(1.1%qtr, +0.24ppt) with rents and dwellings both increasing 1.0% in the quarter and utilities lifting
1.2%. Food was also slightly stronger than expected (0.7%qtr, +0.12ppt) and while not significant to the
overall result it is interesting to track the ongoing rise in motor vehicle prices (0.3%qtr) despite a very
competitive market with Chinese manufacturers rapidly gaining market share over legacy brands.

It is worth noting that food was stronger than we expected (0.7% vs 0.5%) with a robust 1.7% increase
in restaurant meals and a solid 1.2% increase in take away & fast foods outweighing the –0.4% decline
in fresh fruit & vegetables and meat & seafoods. Overall, market services ex volatile prices increased a
very solid 1.2%qtr but with the annual pace easing to 3.2%yr from 3.36%yr.
Outside of housing the other main downside surprise in the quarter was the –0.6% fall in clothing &
footwear (+0.3% expected) with prices falling across just about all categories of garments, footwear
and accessories.
September Quarter Trimmed Mean

### Page 9

Recall that the TM is calculated from seasonally adjust consumption expenditure classes so any
numbers quoted here may not be the same as the original estimates quoted from the CPI. The TM gain
of 1.0% in the quarter was slightly stronger than we had expected even though dwellings (which have a
weight of almost 11% in the TM) was a touch softer at 1.0% compared to the 1.1% we had pencilled in.
Rents (1.0%, 10% weight) also made a solid contribution as did restaurant meals (1.7%qr, 6% weight),

take away & fast food (1.2%, 4% weight), other financial service (1.0%, 6% weight). Domestic travel
was in the TM (1.0%, 4% weight) as was medical & hospital service (1.0%, weight 5%). The range of
the TM was from 0.0% (motor vehicles) to 1.7% (beer).
What does this mean for our December quarter forecast and into 2027?
We are working though the monthly results to see what changes they may imply for individual
component inflationary momentum. Our preliminary analysis suggests that the softer trend in dwellings

is not enough, on its own, to change our Q4 CPI forecast of 1.3%. It does, however, suggest that if all
else held equal (which is not the case as there is likely to be some offsetting stronger gains elsewhere)
the moderation in dwelling inflation presents a very modest downside risk our Q4 TM estimate of 0.9%.
We are still working the numbers and will publish an updated inflation profile as soon as we can. In
addition, the overall momentum in underlying inflationary pressures suggest the downside risk to the

TM from dwellings is more likely to appear in 2027 than in the last quarter of 2026.

The scenario, forecasts and nowcasts of the Economic Research

2
